> ﻿Offer Logistics For Scale
> Prelude:
> What I’m about to explain is the sole reason we have generated the below results:
> * We’ve scaled from 30k/m to $700k/m in around 18 months and we deliver the best client results in our industry. No one comes close in our market.
> * An ecomm agency client scaled from $20k/m to (last month) $300k/m
> * A service based agency client scaled from $140k/m to $600k/m
> * A SaaS client scaled from $1M/m to $2M/m (roughly)
> * We’ve had 50 or so clients scale past $100k/m
>    * And we have about 15 or so clients above $1M/month
> 
> 
> And when I say this is the “sole reason”, I’m assuming that your model is scalable, you have a good product/service, etc. Which if you’re in this program you most likely do and so by extension what I’m about to explain will be what will get you to $100k/m, $500k/m or $1M/m+ through ads (depending on where you’re starting from).
> 
> 
> Also: The job of marketing is to get strangers who are in our target market to book a sales call. The job of the sales call (sales) is to sell that stranger on the product/service.
> 
> 
> This means that in marketing, we do not and should not explain or be ultra clear on the specifics of the offer/product/deliverables (that is for the sales call).
> 
> 
> In other words, if in our ads we said we will “run Facebook ads for you”... you would have never clicked and booked a call with us.
> Important Note:
> Our goal is not to get you sales calls… our goal is to take you from $100k/m to $250k/m to $500k/m or from $20k/m to $200k/m or from $1M/month to $2M/month and we focus on nothing other than exactly what is going to get you to that goal.
> 
> 
> We aren’t ever going to suggest something that is only going to get you sales calls, but not closes or something that is only going to get you closes but then cause fulfilment issues/doesn’t generate you recurring back-end clients.
> 
> 
> Reason being is because that won’t ultimately scale your business, it’ll just cause a few closes and then a bunch of refunds, or a few sales calls but no closes and ultimately lost money. Not the goal.
> 
> 
> The only way to scale is via having a profitable front-end acquisition with a fulfilment model that actually delivers client results which they love and makes them retain with you.
> 
> 
> And everything we do for you/work with you on is to achieve that goal and actually scale your agency/coaching/consulting business.
> Here’s Our Philosophy/Process On Creating Offers
> (And what you need to understand prior to your onboarding call)
> Step 1: Focusing The Ads On Your Most Ideal Top Of The Market Clients.
> 
> 
> The reason why is because if you speak to the top, you naturally get the top and everyone below. If you speak to the bottom, you only get the bottom:
>    *   
> 
> This concept also ties into your offer.
> 
> 
> We don’t want to focus your offer around what you can deliver to the whole market but rather what is the outcome (result) you can achieve for your most ideal, qualified, high-end client who has every resource you require.
> 
> 
> Often times these resources refer to:
>    * Adspend
>    * Location size (if local)
>    * Team size
>    * Call leads immediately
>    * Do everything you require them to do
>    * Make any changes you recommend/require
>    * Get assets/changes/access/creatives, etc turned around ASAP (not weeks or months)
>    * Search volume (if Google Ads)
>    * Traffic volume onto the website
>    * x%+ conversion rate
>    * You must spend at least $X on these creatives/funnels/chatbots, etc
> 
> 
> Literally any requirement you need, we assume in your ads they have them all and THAT is who we create the offer and the outcome for NOT people who have less adspend or don’t call leads immediately, etc. 
> 
> 
> This is how you will always get the highest volume + quality at the cheapest cost through your ads at the highest scale.
> 
> 
> Hence why this is the number one key to unlocking massive scale for your business.
> 
> 
> And most likely the reason you’re a client right now is because you saw one of our ads with a big claim (that’s only for people who have a certain amount of adspend) and what you ultimately bought is a more tailored solution that fits exactly what you need (even though it’s different from what you saw in the ad).
> 
> 
> 90% of people who buy our offer do not buy the offer they saw in the ad. They buy our downsell.
> Step 2: Downselling The Guarantee
> 
> 
> The question I know you’re asking right now is:
> 
> 
> “What if they don’t have (requirements you need)? I can only achieve that outcome (referring to the offer) if they have (requirements).”
> 
> 
> This is where the idea of “downselling the guarantee” comes into play.
> 
> 
> Before I explain this concept, you might be thinking “why would you still try to sell someone who isn’t a perfect, top of the market fit?”
> 
> 
> Well here’s the thing, if for example your ideal client is someone making $100k/m… but you have case studies of clients at $500k/m then we’re always going to focus the offer around the people at $500k/m (top of the market)…
> 
> 
> Naturally, this offer will attract people at 200k/m or 100k/m, so in this instance they’re still a qualified client which you would still want to sell (just not on the offer they saw in the ad as that’s centered around people at $500k/m - so you would downsell them)...
> 
> 
> But also the offer in this example would attract people at $50k/m or $80k/m, etc and while this may be a complete no go for you, that’s fine… but for most offers and what I lead my sales and marketing team on (internally) is the following:
> 
> 
> We should be closing anyone who:
>    * Is in our niche
>    * Has the problem we solve
>    * Has the money to pay us
> 
> 
> Why do we have this mindset even though ideally we only want to work with people above $25k/m or so?
> 
> 
> Simple. At the point of recording this, our immediate marketing goal is to get to $10k/day spend and the only way we can get there is to close anyone who is in our niche and is closable and that we can 100% help (even if they’re not at our ideal $25k+/month revenue range).
> 
> 
> However, this completely depends on how aggressive you want to be. You could set the “bottom” of your market (refer to image above) as your qualified client and the top would be your hyper qualified client (use example from above) and this would scale well to $2-3k/day adspend…
> 
> 
> Or you could broaden your market to essentially be anyone who has the problem you solve and has the money to pay you who’s also in your niche.
> 
> 
> We can of course dive deeper into this on your onboarding call.
> 
> 
> Either way, you will still have the following happen where a qualified prospect will see an ad that has a great offer so they book a call and show up… but on the call, they realize they don’t have the resources required to be able to receive the guarantee/outcome they saw in the ad because they don’t fit that “top of the triangle” criteria…
> 
> 
> But, they do have the problem you solve, they do have the funds to pay you, they are in your niche and they are at a level in which you can help (going back to the $100k vs $500k/m example)…
> 
> 
> This is where we Downsell The Guarantee.
> 
> 
> Here’s the “how to”:
> 
> 
> To effectively downsell a guarantee and get every prospect to say yes to the downsell, you need 2 things:
> 
>    1. A logical explanation as to why they’re not a good fit for the main guarantee (the one they saw in the ad)...
>    1. “Yeah that makes sense”
>    2. Then you downsell to something the prospect still wants…
>    1. If you downsell to no offer or guarantee (as an example) no one will ever close.
> 
> 
> For example:
> 
> 
>    * SCIO:
>    * Ad says 90 qualified sales calls per month or you don’t pay…
>    * 90% of our clients buy our downsell guarantee which is 50 qualified booked calls in 90 days…
>    * Logical explanation:
>    * “As you know, guarantees are always based on worst case scenario, and so in this case, our absolute worst-case scenario would be a $200 cost per booked call… and so to guarantee you 90 qualified calls per month, or in other words, 3 qualified calls per day, we require a $600/day ad spend. Now I know you mentioned your ad budget wasn’t at $18k/month and so here’s what we can do… we can guarantee you 50 qualified sales calls in 90 days which only requires a $100/day ad spend.”
>    * If you run ads then usually “adspend” is your logical explanation for why they aren’t a good fit for the main offer, however, if you have a different service that isn’t ads, ask yourself:
>    * “What’s the thing that if they did have it, they would be eligible for the offer?” 
>    * Inverse and that becomes your logical explanation.
>    * Example: One of our clients had a requirement of needing a 2M population service area in order for him to deliver the results mentioned in his ads. So his logical reasoning became:
>    * In order for us to do XYZ (offer stated in ad), we require you to be servicing a population area of at least 2M, now given you’re in X area with only 700,000 people in it, here’s what we can do (insert your downsell that they still find desirable and WANT which you can deliver given their resources).
>    * We can help you with this logical explanation + downsell offer on your onboarding call, I just want you thinking about it for now.
> Step 3: Determining The Terms
> Remember: you are in complete control over the terms you set for your guarantees. If you have a bigger guarantee that is more difficult for you to deliver then simply increase your price on that particular offer. 
> 
> 
> Here’s how we structure it (again, this is what took us from $30k/m to $700k/m in 18 months and is what helps us deliver the best client results in our industry).
> 
> 
> ScaleClients.io:
>    * The guarantee almost all (90%) of our clients buy is the 50 qualified calls booked in 90 days offer. However, if we ran this guarantee in our marketing (ads) it wouldn’t generate any calls because 1. It’s not desirable and 2. It’s completely saturated.
>    * So instead, we run the 15 clients/month guaranteed offer.
>    * However, this is a lot more time intensive and has a lot more requirements in order for us to deliver it. So here are the terms we set:
>    * We charge $40k for 4 months and…
>    * The client must have at least $30k - $50k/month in adspend for the full 3 months - depending on the price of their offer ($90k - $150k in total adspend)
>    * Or, they can take our downsell - 50 qualified calls in 90 days - (a result they still want) which has the following terms:
>    * We charge $17.5k for 4 months and…
>    * They can get started with $100/day in adspend and we can scale spend up or down whenever they would like...
>    * See how based on the terms we set, most clients take our downsell offer.
> 
> 
> Keep in mind, at no point do we ever bait and switch the prospect. The offer they saw in the ad, they can 100% buy. They just need to be able to meet the terms we set.
> 
> 
> You never want to lie in your ads and/or bait and switch. This will come back to bite you. I see so many of our competitors trying to copy us but they don’t understand this backend structure so they end up outright lying and they get taken down and never build a real, long lasting business that scales.
> Step 4: Conditional Refund Guarantee Stipulations
> Ask yourself: “In order for a client to be able to generate (outcome) what exactly do they need/need to do?”
> 
> 
> Or, you can look at your biggest clients who have gotten the biggest results with you, and retrace exactly what they did to get those results.
> 
> 
> In either case, whatever your clients need to do to get the result, that’s exactly what you need to put in your contract stipulations in order for your clients to be eligible for the refund.
> 
> 
> These stipulations can be as specific as:
>    * You must call all leads within the first 3 hours of them opting in
>    * You must spend (minimum) $500/day consecutively for 90 days
>    * You must send through a call recording of every sales call you take
>    * You must implement on every ammendment/change we say
>    * At the end of each day you need to send us a full breakdown on every lead you spoke with and exactly what the outcome of the call/conversation was (this must be done at the end of every day consecutively NOT at the end of weeks or months)
>    * You must return all new creatives to us within 7 days of us sending them over to you
> 
> 
> Again, remember, you are in complete control over the stipulations you set. You can put any requirements you need into your contract.
> 
> 
> ScaleClients.io Example:
>   
> 
> Side Benefit:
> One thing I found by adding these stipulations into our contract is that it INCREASES client results because more clients do the required actions to be successful because they want to be eligible for the refund…
> 
> 
> But becase they do those actions, they end up getting the result and so you don’t need to refund… win-win.
> 
> 
> ________________
> 
> 
> If you’re still apprehensive about doing a guarantee, remember this (from Hormozi)...
> 
> 
> If you don’t have a guarantee and you close 10 clients per month, adding a guarantee in can bump you to 13 clients per month… if you have to refund 1 per month (because of the guarantee)... net net, you’re still up 2 new clients per month that you wouldn’t have otherwise gotten.
> 
> 
> However, from my experience, guarantees never have a 30% increase, at minimum, I’ve found them to have a 100-200% increase (2-3x).
> 
> 
> ________________
> 
> 
> 
> 
> Everything we do at SCIO is to get our clients the best possible results and give them the best possible experience.
> 
> 
> And this is ultimately how we achieve that at such a high scale and it’s how you can to with your agency/coaching/consulting biz.
> 
> 
> On your onboarding call we’ll go back through this with you and we’ll create your offer for you based on everything I’ve covered in this training.
> 
> 
> And remember, everything we do for you is to scale you to $100k/m, $500k/m, $1M/m+ and beyond, we’re on your team and in this with you. Whatever you need, we’re here for you.

> # 【0†Credit Ascend Offer x ScaleClients.io.txt†file_000000009bc071f58b1f6949120f5fb5】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: Credit Ascend Offer x ScaleClients.io.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn7file0
> Mclick Target: "7:0"
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] I help small business owners with strong credit (720-750+ with multiple tradelines & solid limits) learn how to get up to $250K+ in funding to help them grow and scale. 
> [L2] 
> [L3] 1) My program will teach people on how to fund themselves from scratch, leveraging their credit; and how to leverage the system most optimally to get lending from the banks, without relying on any funding companies & their fees, while learning how to do the whole process A-Z themselves. 
> [L4] 
> [L5] 2) My promise, is that if they have a qualified profile, and take verified action on a proven system, that they will get $50-$100K+ in as soon as weeks to months, depending on their  state and execution of the steps. If they are not able to generate at least $50K+ minimum in new funding, I will refund them, or work with them for free until they achieve $100K+.
> [L6] 
> [L7] 3) Terms & Pricepoints are $5K-$10K for access to a strategic  course program with live group coaching calls, where if they complete the course, apply the steps (verifiable), and participate in the coaching calls, while executing on the action plan & asking questions; they will inevitably achieve results. If they show up and match our 100% effort in helping them, and an anomaly occurs. They are honorably entitled to a refund, as we are here to genuinely help people, and not just steal their money. (I've considered opening up a lower tier offer at a memberhship price of $97/mo for those that need assistance with cleaning up their credit (DIY education w/ a group coaching call weekly. - this would then be a longer term cycle to nuture these clients to becoming qualified, and then engaging in the higher tiered funding program) 
> [L8] - I've also considered a 3-6 month membership access to the live coaching calls / program, where afterwards, if they want to still retain access to the community and receive extended support for future funding rounds, they can stay for a monthly rate (undetermined YET for the higher ticket offer members)
> [L9] 
> [L10] Current market either offers 
> [L11] - $0 upfront, DFY funding, 10% backend performance fee
> [L12] - $500-$1000 upfront, DFY funding, 7.5-10% backend performance fee
> [L13] - $5-8K one time DFY customized funding 
> [L14] - $15-$30K one time + monthly fee 6-12 months later for DIY funding with 3x weekly coaching call support for Fixing credit, building credit, and funding. +1 on 1 calls and monthly mastermind for $30K program
> [L15] 
> [L16] My intention is to be able to teach people how to fund themselves better, with more detail (focused on people actually succeeding) than the $15K-$30K program, but at a cheaper price point. 
> [L17] - exploring hybridized DIY/DWY/DFY potentials
> [L18] - The main thing is that I know I can confidently record videos and share with people everything they need to know to build and clean a credit profile attractive to the banks to receive lending. With much insider knowledge of how operations are, and  what the banks & underwriters are looking for. (Things most people have no clue about how to do, where the normal process can naturally result in 10x+ lower limits, and up to 4x+ longer time frames for the inexperienced.
> [L19] - although not the main focus of the funding offer, i do also know how people can remove/clean negatives from their credit report, like collections, charge offs, late payments, hard inquiries, negative accounts, etc.

> # 【2†Offer Logistics For Scale.txt†file_000000003050722f9362b24c4f3ec29b】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: Offer Logistics For Scale.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn7file2
> Mclick Target: "7:2"
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] This is how you will always get the highest volume + quality at the cheapest cost through your ads at the highest scale.
> [L2] 
> [L3] 
> [L4] Hence why this is the number one key to unlocking massive scale for your business.
> [L5] 
> [L6] 
> [L7] And most likely the reason you’re a client right now is because you saw one of our ads with a big claim (that’s only for people who have a certain amount of adspend) and what you ultimately bought is a more tailored solution that fits exactly what you need (even though it’s different from what you saw in the ad).
> [L8] 
> [L9] 
> [L10] 90% of people who buy our offer do not buy the offer they saw in the ad. They buy our downsell.
> [L11] Step 2: Downselling The Guarantee
> [L12] 
> [L13] 
> [L14] The question I know you’re asking right now is:
> [L15] 
> [L16] 
> [L17] “What if they don’t have (requirements you need)? I can only achieve that outcome (referring to the offer) if they have (requirements).”
> [L18] 
> [L19] 
> [L20] This is where the idea of “downselling the guarantee” comes into play.
> [L21] 
> [L22] 
> [L23] Before I explain this concept, you might be thinking “why would you still try to sell someone who isn’t a perfect, top of the market fit?”
> [L24] 
> [L25] 
> [L26] Well here’s the thing, if for example your ideal client is someone making $100k/m… but you have case studies of clients at $500k/m then we’re always going to focus the offer around the people at $500k/m (top of the market)…
> [L27] 
> [L28] 
> [L29] Naturally, this offer will attract people at 200k/m or 100k/m, so in this instance they’re still a qualified client which you would still want to sell (just not on the offer they saw in the ad as that’s centered around people at $500k/m - so you would downsell them).
> [L30] 
> [L31] 
> [L32] But also the offer in this example would attract people at $50k/m or $80k/m, etc and while this may be a complete no go for you, that’s fine… but for most offers and what I lead my sales and marketing team on (internally) is the following:
> [L33] 
> [L34] 
> [L35] We should be closing anyone who:
> [L36]    * Is in our niche
> [L37]    * Has the problem we solve
> [L38]    * Has the money to pay us
> [L39] 
> [L40] 
> [L41] Why do we have this mindset even though ideally we only want to work with people above $25k/m or so?
> [L42] 
> [L43] 
> [L44] Simple. At the point of recording this, our immediate marketing goal is to get to $10k/day spend and the only way we can get there is to close anyone who is in our niche and is closable and that we can 100% help (even if they’re not at our ideal $25k+/month revenue range).
> [L45] 
> [L46] 
> [L47] However, this completely depends on how aggressive you want to be. You could set the “bottom” of your market (refer to image above) as your qualified client and the top would be your hyper qualified client (use example from above) and this would scale well to $2-3k/day adspend…
> [L48] 
> [L49] 
> [L50] Or you could broaden your market to essentially be anyone who has the problem you solve and has the money to pay you who’s also in your niche.
> [L51] 
> [L52] 
> [L53] We can of course dive deeper into this on your onboarding call.

> # 【5†Offer Logistics For Scale.txt†file_000000003050722f9362b24c4f3ec29b】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: Offer Logistics For Scale.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn7file5
> Mclick Target: "7:5"
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] In other words, if in our ads we said we will “run Facebook ads for you”... you would have never clicked and booked a call with us.
> [L2] Important Note:
> [L3] Our goal is not to get you sales calls… our goal is to take you from $100k/m to $250k/m to $500k/m or from $20k/m to $200k/m or from $1M/month to $2M/month and we focus on nothing other than exactly what is going to get you to that goal.
> [L4] 
> [L5] 
> [L6] We aren’t ever going to suggest something that is only going to get you sales calls, but not closes or something that is only going to get you closes but then cause fulfilment issues/doesn’t generate you recurring back-end clients.
> [L7] 
> [L8] 
> [L9] Reason being is because that won’t ultimately scale your business, it’ll just cause a few closes and then a bunch of refunds, or a few sales calls but no closes and ultimately lost money. Not the goal.
> [L10] 
> [L11] 
> [L12] The only way to scale is via having a profitable front-end acquisition with a fulfilment model that actually delivers client results which they love and makes them retain with you.
> [L13] 
> [L14] 
> [L15] And everything we do for you/work with you on is to achieve that goal and actually scale your agency/coaching/consulting business.
> [L16] Here’s Our Philosophy/Process On Creating Offers
> [L17] (And what you need to understand prior to your onboarding call)
> [L18] Step 1: Focusing The Ads On Your Most Ideal Top Of The Market Clients.
> [L19] 
> [L20] 
> [L21] The reason why is because if you speak to the top, you naturally get the top and everyone below. If you speak to the bottom, you only get the bottom:
> [L22]    *   
> [L23] 
> [L24] This concept also ties into your offer.
> [L25] 
> [L26] 
> [L27] We don’t want to focus your offer around what you can deliver to the whole market but rather what is the outcome (result) you can achieve for your most ideal, qualified, high-end client who has every resource you require.
> [L28] 
> [L29] 
> [L30] Often times these resources refer to:
> [L31]    * Adspend
> [L32]    * Location size (if local)
> [L33]    * Team size
> [L34]    * Call leads immediately
> [L35]    * Do everything you require them to do
> [L36]    * Make any changes you recommend/require
> [L37]    * Get assets/changes/access/creatives, etc turned around ASAP (not weeks or months)
> [L38]    * Search volume (if Google Ads)
> [L39]    * Traffic volume onto the website
> [L40]    * x%+ conversion rate
> [L41]    * You must spend at least $X on these creatives/funnels/chatbots, etc
> [L42] 
> [L43] 
> [L44] Literally any requirement you need, we assume in your ads they have them all and THAT is who we create the offer and the outcome for NOT people who have less adspend or don’t call leads immediately, etc. 
> [L45] 
> [L46] 
> [L47] This is how you will always get the highest volume + quality at the cheapest cost through your ads at the highest scale.
> [L48] 
> [L49] 
> [L50] Hence why this is the number one key to unlocking massive scale for your business.
> [L51] 
> [L52] 
> [L53] And most likely the reason you’re a client right now is because you saw one of our ads with a big claim (that’s only for people who have a certain amount of adspend) and what you ultimately bought is a more tailored solution that fits exactly what you need (even though it’s different from what you saw in the ad).
> [L54] 
> [L55] 
> [L56] 90% of people who buy our offer do not buy the offer they saw in the ad. They buy our downsell.
> [L57] Step 2: Downselling The Guarantee
> [L58] 
> [L59] 
> [L60] The question I know you’re asking right now is:
> [L61] 
> [L62] 
> [L63] “What if they don’t have (requirements you need)? I can only achieve that outcome (referring to the offer) if they have (requirements).”
> [L64] 
> [L65] 
> [L66] This is where the idea of “downselling the guarantee” comes into play.
> [L67] 
> [L68] 
> [L69] Before I explain this concept, you might be thinking “why would you still try to sell someone who isn’t a perfect,

> [L1] ﻿Offer Logistics For Scale
> [L2] Prelude:
> [L3] What I’m about to explain is the sole reason we have generated the below results:
> [L4] * We’ve scaled from 30k/m to $700k/m in around 18 months and we deliver the best client results in our industry. No one comes close in our market.
> [L5] * An ecomm agency client scaled from $20k/m to (last month) $300k/m
> [L6] * A service based agency client scaled from $140k/m to $600k/m
> [L7] * A SaaS client scaled from $1M/m to $2M/m (roughly)
> [L8] * We’ve had 50 or so clients scale past $100k/m
> [L9]    * And we have about 15 or so clients above $1M/month
> [L10] 
> [L11] 
> [L12] And when I say this is the “sole reason”, I’m assuming that your model is scalable, you have a good product/service, etc. Which if you’re in this program you most likely do and so by extension what I’m about to explain will be what will get you to $100k/m, $500k/m or $1M/m+ through ads (depending on where you’re starting from).
> [L13] 
> [L14] 
> [L15] Also: The job of marketing is to get strangers who are in our target market to book a sales call. The job of the sales call (sales) is to sell that stranger on the product/service.
> [L16] 
> [L17] 
> [L18] This means that in marketing, we do not and should not explain or be ultra clear on the specifics of the offer/product/deliverables (that is for the sales call).
> [L19] 
> [L20] 
> [L21] In other words, if in our ads we said we will “run Facebook ads for you”... you would have never clicked and booked a call with us.
> [L22] Important Note:
> [L23] Our goal is not to get you sales calls… our goal is to take you from $100k/m to $250k/m to $500k/m or from $20k/m to $200k/m or from $1M/month to $2M/month and we focus on nothing other than exactly what is going to get you to that goal.
> [L24] 
> [L25] 
> [L26] We aren’t ever going to suggest something that is only going to get you sales calls, but not closes or something that is only going to get you closes but then cause fulfilment issues/doesn’t generate you recurring back-end clients.
> [L27] 
> [L28] 
> [L29] Reason being is because that won’t ultimately scale your business, it’ll just cause a few closes and then a bunch of refunds, or a few sales calls but no closes and ultimately lost money. Not the goal.
> [L30] 
> [L31] 
> [L32] The only way to scale is via having a profitable front-end acquisition with a fulfilment model that actually delivers client results which they love and makes them retain with you.
> [L33] 
> [L34] 
> [L35] And everything we do for you/work with you on is to achieve that goal and actually scale your agency/coaching/consulting business.
> [L36] Here’s Our Philosophy/Process On Creating Offers
> [L37] (And what you need to understand prior to your onboarding call)

> [L39] 
> [L40]

> [L42]    *

> [L1] How To Use Low-Budget AI Meta Ads To Grow To $100k/mo+ (ScaleClients.io Offer Walkthrough)
> [L2] 
> [L3] 
> [L4] Kai Bax
> [L5] 
> [L6] In this video, I want to walk through how to use lowbudget AI meta ads to grow any marketing into your coaching business to 10K per month, 100K per month, or even a million a month plus in the next 90 days or less for as little as $400 per month. And for some context on me really quick before I go into it, my name's Kai Baxton. Just so you know, I've actually done what I'm talking about here. I started in the industry 7 years ago at 17 years old. And by the time I turned 18, I hit my first 100K a month with my e-commerce marketing agency. And then I transitioned into a coaching business and scaled that to 250K a month because I thought the only way to scale was through coaching. I was wrong on that, but I did it anyway. And then now I'm the CEO of a done for you hybrid consulting marketing company which does about a million per month in revenue as you can see here. And on top of that, more importantly, right now, I'm spending 150K per month, give or take, on my own ads, which generate my own sales team, 3 to 500 qualified sales calls per month at between $250 to $300 cost per qualified booked call for our back-end high ticket 20K offer. And you can see that spend right here is split between a couple accounts. Um, needless to say, it's extremely profitable. And more importantly, this is the thing. We've been able to replicate this across hundreds of other coaches, consultants, and agencies over the past four to five years, including some of the biggest names in the industry, such as Russfino, 7M Media, Grow AI, Ecom Capital, Phase One Ventures, The Draw Shop, LifeLife Media, among thousands of others. For example, one of our clients here, Matt, has closed $611,000 so far in his first 57 days of running his ads. Last month, Nate and his sales team had 130 showed up sales calls. They closed 42 new clients and did 272K in revenue again from the same lowbudget AI meta ads. Paul, same again. 276K in new cash collected in his first 90 days of working with us. This was an older screenshot. This time he'd only done 256, but same same. Uh Jason did 90K last week, cash collected, and he's on pace to do 500k this month with his BTOC coaching offer. Jorge closed eight new clients at 25K per client, totaling 200k in new revenue closed in the first 16 days of this month. Steven's consistently doing 70k profit per week with his B2B consulting business. And last week he did uh 13 closes. James from the ecom bar scale from 50k a month to 20k a month in his first 30 days of of uh of running these lowbudget ads for his high ticket BTOC coaching offer. Uh Blake from the Agency Collective closed 14 new clients at 9K per client in the first 20 days of this month, totaling 100K in new MR for his B2C coaching offer. Summer closed six new deals just last week with 47K in cash collected for her coaching business. Again, that was just last week. Uh Jacob here has collected 75K cash with 125K in contracted revenue this month from only 5K in ad spend as you can see here.

> # 【12†HOW TO MAKE A GRAND SLAM OFFER Prob.txt†file_00000000a8e471f5ad43f1268dce94ce】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: HOW TO MAKE A GRAND SLAM OFFER Prob.txt
> Citation Marker: fileciteturn7file12
> Mclick Target: "7:12"
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] The next month, the $5,000 that comes in would be your first profitable month, and each month thereafter would be profitable (assuming they all stay). This is an example of a commoditized service — normal agency work. There’s a million of them, and they all look the same. Commoditized businesses and offers have a harder time getting responses from ads because all their marketing looks the same as everyone else's.   It’s reasonable, but it’s easily duplicated (and subject to commoditization). This commoditization creates a price-driven purchase . . . 
> [L2] 
> [L3] You are forced to be priced “competitively” to get clients and to stay that way to keep them. If the client sees a cheaper version of the “same thing,” then the value discrepancy will cause them to swap providers. This is a dilemma . . . 
> [L4] 
> [L5] lose this client, the rest of your clients, and potential clients, or stay “competitive.” Your margins become so thin they vanish. 
> [L6] 
> [L7] Furthermore, it’s hard to get prospects to say yes (and keep them saying yes) unless you’re hypervigilant about clients commoditizing your business by staying “competitive.” And that’s the problem with the old commoditized way. They’re able to compare. 
> [L8] 
> [L9] Unless you switch to a Grand Slam Offer, your prices will keep getting beaten down. The business eventually dies, or the entrepreneur throws in the towel. No bueno. We want to make an offer that’s so different that you can skip the awkward explanation of why your product is different from everyone else’s (which, if they have to ask, then they are probably too ignorant to understand the explanation) and instead just have the offer do that work for you. That’s the Grand Slam Offer way. Let’s dive in to see the contrast in sales numbers. New Grand Slam Offer Way (Differentiated, Incomparable) (Value-Driven) Grand Slam Offer: Pay one time. (No recurring fee. No retainer.) Just cover ad spend. I’ll generate leads and work your leads for you. And only pay me if people show up. And I’ll guarantee you get 20 people in your first month, or you get your next month free. I’ll also provide all the best practices from other businesses like yours. 
> [L10] 
> [L11] ●Daily sales coaching for your staff 
> [L12] ●Tested scripts 
> [L13] ●Tested price points and offers to swipe and deploy 
> [L14] ●Sales recordings . . . and everything else you need to sell and fulfill your customers. 
> [L15] 
> [L16] I’ll give you the entire play book for (insert industry), absolutely free just for becoming a client. In a nutshell, I'm feeding people into your business, showing you, exactly, how to sell them so that you can get the highest prices, which means that you make the most money possible . . . sound fair enough? 
> [L17] 
> [L18] It’s clear these are drastically different offers . . . but so what? Where’s the money!? Let’s compare both in the below chart.       Breakdown: You spend the same amount of money for the same eyeballs. Then, you get 2.5x more people to respond to your advertisement because it’s a more compelling offer. From there, you close 2.5x as many people because the offer is so much more compelling. From there, you are able to charge a 4x higher price up front. The end result is 2.5 x 2.5 x 4 = 22.4x more cash collected up front. 
> [L19] 
> [L20] Yes, you spent $10,000 to make $112,000. You just made money getting new customers.
> # 【13†HOW TO MAKE A GRAND SLAM OFFER Prob.txt†file_00000000a8e471f5ad43f1268dce94ce】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: HOW TO MAKE A GRAND SLAM OFFER Prob.txt

> Mclick Target: "7:13"
> Content Snippet:
> [L1] For each guarantee I
> [L2] will walk though: the "if", the "what they get," my take, and scripting in the real world.
> [L3] 
> [L4] 1) Unconditional "No Questions Asked" Refund Guarantees
> [L5] 
> [L6] Unconditional are the strongest guarantees. They're basically a trial where they pay first then see if they
> [L7] like it. This gets a LOT more people to buy, but you will have some people request refunds.
> [L8] 
> [L9] Guarantee: If you don't achieve X, in Y time, we will [insert offer] ...
> [L10] What the Client Gets: A) a full refund, B) a 50 percent refund, C) a refund of their ad spend and any
> [L11] ancillary costs incurred, D) you pay for a competitor's program instead, E) you return their money plus
> [L12] an additional $1,000 (or other applicable amount)
> [L13] My Take: This is the strongest guarantee. But, it can be risky. You put yourself in a situation where if
> [L14] someone does not achieve the results, whether because of your fault or not, you're held accountable. So
> [L15] do the math. Not recommended for offers with high unrecoverable costs to fulfill.
> [L16] 
> [L17] Scripting: This is the best wording I've seen of an unconditional guarantee. I copied this wording from
> [L18] Jason Fladlien (with permission).
> [L19] 
> [L20] 
> [L21] "I'm not asking you to decide yes or no today ... I'm asking you to make a fully informed decision, that is all.
> [L22] The only way you can make a fully informed decision is on the inside, not the outside. So you get on the inside
> [L23] and see if everything we say on this webinar is true and valuable to you. Then, if it is, that's when you decide
> [L24] to keep it. If it's not for you, no hard feelings. You will then, after signing up at URL be able to make a fully
> [L25] informed decision that this isn't for you. But you can't make this decision right now for the same reason you
> [L26] don't buy a house without first looking at the inside of it. And know this ... whether it's 29 min or 29 days from
> [L27] now ... if you ain't happy, I ain't happy. For any reason whatsoever, if you want your money back you can get it
> [L28] because I only want to keep your money if you're happy. All you have to do is go to support@xyz.com and tell
> [L29] us "gimme my money" and you got it, and in short order - our response times to any support request average 61
> [L30] min over a 24/7 time period. You can only make such a guarantee when you're confident that what you have
> [L31] is the real deal and I'm fairly confident that when you sign up at URL you're getting exactly what you need to
> [L32] BENEFIT."
> [L33] 
> [L34] [Alternate] "Satisfaction" Refund Guarantee:
> [L35] 
> [L36] What the Client Gets: If at any time they're not satisfied with the level of service they're receiving from
> [L37] you, they can request a refund (at any time) for the program.
> [L38] 
> [L39] My Take: This was my guarantee when I sold weight-loss programs. Besides being an irresistible offer,
> [L40] I guaranteed satisfaction (not results). I used the strength of my guarantee to close a lot of deals. If you
> [L41] are good at what you do, you can use a guarantee like this to push a lot of people over the edge. The lines
> [L42] below made me a lot of money. I had two people take me up on it out of 4,000 sales in three and a half
> [L43] years.
> [L44] 
> [L45] Scripting: "Do you think I'd still be in business if I gave a crazy guarantee like that and wasn't good at what I
> [L46] did?

> [L1] Right.
> [L2] It would work. It would also be a nightmare. So I would probably put contingencies like, "Provided
> [L3] you've already spent $10,000 on your existing offer using our structure, the offer you ran was for lead
> [L4] generation, and it was a free offer. These are things that would make it unlikely they would not succeed.
> [L5] If for some reason they hadn't with those stipulations in place, I could probably fix their problem in a few
> [L6] minutes just looking at it - which lowers my risk.
> [L7] 
> [L8] 2f) Hotel + Airfare Perks Guarantee
> [L9] What the Client Gets: If you don't receive value, we will reimburse your product and your hotel + airfare.
> [L10] 
> [L11] My Take: This is technically a "refund of ancillary costs" from our first example. I just love it a lot for
> [L12] workshops and in-person experiences. Normally the event would cost more than the hotel and airfare, so
> [L13] it's like adding an extra $1000 to a guarantee but way more tangible. It's original enough that people like
> [L14] it.
> [L15] 
> [L16] 2g) Wage-Payment Guarantee
> [L17] 
> [L18] What the Client Gets: You offer to pay their hourly rate, whatever that may be, if they don't find your
> [L19] call/session with them valuable.
> [L20] 
> [L21] My Take: This is also an ancillary cost guarantee. If someone ever asks for the wage payment, just ask
> [L22] them for their tax return and divide it by 1,960 (number of working hours at 40 hrs/wk for a year). But
> [L23] no one asking for a refund will actually do that, so you will never actually have to give one of these out.
> [L24] Like ever.
> [L25] 
> [L26] 2h) Release of Service Guarantee
> [L27] 
> [L28] What the Client Gets: You let them out of their contract free of charge.
> [L29] 
> [L30] My Take: This voids a commitment or cancellation fee. If you have a business that has enforceable
> [L31] commitments, contracts or clauses, this can be a powerful guarantee. Better yet, if you are in a business
> [L32] that does not enforce your contracts, then you have nothing to lose by adding the guarantee.
> [L33] 
> [L34] 2i) Delayed Second Payment Guarantee
> [L35] 
> [L36] What the Client Gets: You won't bill them again until after they make or get their first outcome. Ex: Lose
> [L37] your first five pounds ... make your first sale ... get your website live, etc.
> [L38] 
> [L39] My Take: I like this a lot, especially if you have a very systematized process for getting the first result. It
> [L40] gets the prospect moving. It also focuses your team on activating your client. This is a great one when
> [L41] you know what metric or action drives activation (predicting indicator of long term retention) of a client.
> [L42] I've successfully used this guarantee loads of times.
> [L43] 
> [L44] 2j) First Outcome Guarantee
> [L45] 
> [L46] What the Client Gets: You continue to pay their ancillary costs (ad spend, hotel, etc) until they reach
> [L47] their first outcome. Example: If you don't make your first sale in 14 days, we will pay for your ad spend
> [L48] until you do.
> [L49] 
> [L50] My Take: Just like the delayed second payment, just centered around a different cost. I personally like this
> [L51] setup a lot. It keeps everyone focused on getting that first dollar over the bridge. Once that one comes
> [L52] across, the second comes soon after.
> [L53] 
> [L54] 2k) Prepayment Guarantee
> [L55] 
> [L56] What the Client Gets: You don't need to guarantee everything you sell. Instead, you can choose to
> [L57] guarantee a specific payment plan or option you want someone to take. In this way, a guarantee
> [L58] incentivizes a desired action or commitment.
> [L59] 
> [L60] My Take: This is super effective if you have a product or service many people doubt they'll complete or
> [L61] succeed with. Offering a guarantee as a function of prepayment, gets them even more committed.

> [L4] free of charge. Generally, Y should give them at least twice the duration.

> [L7] involvement. So instead of being on the hook "forever," you're only on the hook for an additional Y period

> [L9] which may be an easier place for you to start before doing the "all out" Service Guarantee above. This

> [L13] 
> [L14] What the Client Gets: You give them back what they paid but in a credit toward any service you offer.

> [L16] My Take: This is best used during an upsell process to seal the deal on a service they are unsure they will
> [L17] like. They already like what they have, you are trying to sell them more of that. Worst case, they can apply
> [L18] it to the thing they already like. So it maintains goodwill with the customer.

## 🤖 ChatGPT

## First video debrief (the core offer logic)
### The mechanism
1) **Build the promise around the TOP buyer** (who can actually get the “big” result). fileciteturn7file5L18-L47  
2) **Gate the big promise with terms** (so it’s true, not hype). fileciteturn7file5L30-L45  
3) **If they don’t meet terms, “downsell the guarantee”** (still close them on a smaller win they still want). fileciteturn7file2L11-L31  
4) **Contract stipulations = refund eligibility** (protects you + forces execution). fileciteturn7file1L17-L46  
5) **Don’t bait/switch**: the big offer must be purchasable if they meet terms. fileciteturn7file1L13-L16  

That’s the “ScaleClients” backend structure you’re referencing. fileciteturn7file1L1-L10

---

## Does your avatar meet Kai’s “3 hidden keys”?
Your current avatar: **720–750+**, clean file, defined use for **$100K–$250K+**, **3–5 hrs/week**, 90-day plan.

### Key #1: Pre-existing desire
**Yes** — they want approvals / high limits to deploy. Your notes already frame “up to $250K+” as the target desire. fileciteturn7file0L1-L6  

### Key #2: One-of-one
**Not yet** — “learn funding A–Z” is valuable, but not *incomparable* by itself because competitors can say “we teach you” too. You need a **unique mechanism + unique deliverable** (we’ll design this).

### Key #3: Proof bigger than promise
**Unknown** — you haven’t anchored the offer to *specific proof assets* yet (case studies, screenshots, lender approvals, before/after profiles, timelines). Kai’s model collapses without this.

So: **Avatar is strong. Offer is not formed yet.**

---

## Clean language fix: “capital” vs “credit”
You’re right: “capital” can imply cash-in-bank. If your outcome is primarily **credit approvals / limits**, use:
- **“Approved credit”**
- **“Deployable credit lines”**
- **“Funding approvals”**
- **“Total approved limits”**

Your own doc already uses “funding” language; stay consistent. fileciteturn7file0L1-L6

---

## The unified formula (harmonized to YOUR credit program)
**TOP-OF-MARKET PROMISE (big, specific) + TERMS (truth gate) + DOWNSOLD GUARANTEE (for everyone else) + STIPULATIONS (execution + refund protection) + UNIQUE MECHANISM (one-of-one) + PROOF (bigger than claim)**  
(All of those pieces are explicitly in the ScaleClients offer logistics + Kai framework.) fileciteturn7file5L18-L56 fileciteturn7file1L17-L46

---

# Apply step-by-step (STARTING NOW): Step 1 = Nail the TOP buyer + what they *actually* want
We are not designing the full offer yet. We’re locking the inputs.

## Step 1A — Define “Top of Market” precisely (your 720–750+ is a good start)
From your notes, your best-fit seems like:  
**Owner/operator with 720–750+, multiple tradelines + strong limits, clean file, wants $100K–$250K+ approvals to deploy.** fileciteturn7file0L1-L6

Now we tighten it into **one sentence that qualifies hard** (prevents junk leads / CPA inflation):
> **“720+ owner/operators who can deploy $100K–$250K in approved credit for a real business plan, and will execute 3–5 hrs/week for 90 days.”**

This aligns with “speak to the top” (so you attract top + some below). fileciteturn7file5L21-L47

## Step 1B — Identify the “heart’s desire” question (Kai’s key #1)
You already have the master prompt:
> “If I could snap my fingers and give you anything you wanted… what would you ask for?”

For THIS market, you need the *exact wording* they say back, because that becomes your headline/offer.

### Your action (fast, zero cost)
Run **10 DMs** (not ads) to your ideal prospects and ask **only these 3 questions**:
1) “If you could snap your fingers and instantly get the *funding outcome* you want, what is it?”  
2) “What have you tried, and why didn’t it work?” (this becomes your “new mechanism” angle)  
3) “What would make you 100% confident it’ll work for you?” (this becomes terms + proof)

This directly feeds your one-of-one mechanism + proof requirements.

---

## Pause (your turn)
Reply with the ONE sentence you want to use as the **top-of-market callout** (tight + simple).  
Example formats (choose one style):
1) “**720+ owners who want $100K–$250K in approvals in 90 days** (with 3–5 hrs/week execution).”
2) “**90-Day $100K+ Approval Sprint** for **720+ owners** (3–5 hrs/week).”
3) “**720+ Credit → $100K+ Approved Limits** in **90 days** (business-use only).”

Once you paste your chosen callout, I’ll move to **Step 2: One-of-one design (unique mechanism + unique deliverable)** and we’ll make it *incomparable* to $0-upfront DFY funders without confusing the market.

> [!abstract]- Tool
> Make sure to include fileciteturn8file0 in your response to cite this file, or to surface it as a link.
> 
> ﻿What Makes The Perfect Offer
> 1. TAM
> We always want to start with the TAM prior to creating your offer as when running ads we need the market we’re going after to be a certain size otherwise we can’t effectively scale.
> 
> 
> TAM = Total Addressable Market
> 
> 
> Meaning, how many people are in the market we’re going after.
> 
> 
> The ideal TAM is more than 30,000 in the US and less than 100,000.
> 
> 
> We want more than 30,000 so that it gives us some good scale on ads, however, we want less than 100,000 because otherwise our messaging is too broad and we need to narrow it down.
> 
> 
> If the market you’re planning on going after is above 100,000 then all we need to do is refine our messaging slightly and go after a segment of the market.
> 
> 
> We can help you with this on your onboarding call if you don’t already have this clearly mapped out.
> 
> 
> This applies to the niche we’re targeting but more importantly, the revenue level (if B2B).
> 
> 
> For example: there may be 50,000 coffee businesses in the US but only 10,000 who make more than $500k/year…
> 
> 
> In this case (if your ideal client is a coffee business making over $500k/year), we would need to expand the niche/call out or go into an adjacent niche which in this instance could be CPG brands.
> 
> 
> Typically, you can find the TAM of almost any market by searching on Google.
> 2. The Outcome
> Once we’ve defined a niche that has a TAM between 30,000 - 100,000, we then need to identify that specific markets (coffee brands making over $500k/year as an example) pre-existing desire.
> 
> 
> Every market segment has a pre-existing desire. 
> 
> 
> When we’re creating offers we’re not trying to create desire in the market, we simply need to look at the market segment and find the pre-existing desire and channel that into the offer we make.
> 
> 
> In other words, you want to optimize your fulfilment (and offer) around your markets pre-existing desires, not try to change the markets desires to fit your fulfilment.
> 
> 
> We want to ask the question:
> 
> 
> “If I were to ask every person in this market segment what they want.. what would 80% or more tell me? In other words, what is at the forefront of their mind every day?”
> 
> 
> The easiest offer framework is: find the markets “broken arm”, take the inverse of that and guarantee that you will give them a big amount of that thing in the immediate future (90-180 days) or they get their money back.
> 
> 
> Example: Agency owners want more qualified sales calls and $3k/month+ clients that retain for 12 months+ that are also great clients to work with.
> 
> 
> Our offer: Marketing agencies above $25k/month: we’ll guarantee you 15 new clients per month on your full priced retainer or you don’t pay and I’ll send you $3k for wasting your time.
> 
> 
> This of course has requirements (as mentioned in the previous training), however, that’s when the logistics/downsell training comes into play.
> 
> 
> Here’s a more formal way of defining the offer:
> 
> 
> * Offer (O) = [N + M(V) + T] - R
>    * Niche (N): Targeted market segment.
>    * Metrics (M): Expected results quantified.
>    * Volume (V): Quantity of results.
>    * Timeframe (T): Expected duration for results.
>    * Risk Reversal (R): Assurances to mitigate client risks.
> 
> 
> Again, all you need to do at this stage is just be thinking about your TAM + offer outcome, you do not need to create this yourself as we will work with you to create the perfect offer on your onboarding call.

# PART 1 — FULL MASTERY BREAKDOWN  
*(What Makes the Perfect Offer)* fileciteturn8file0

This document is deceptively simple. It’s actually a **hard filter system**. Each step *eliminates bad ideas* before you waste time building offers that can’t scale.

---

## STEP 1 — TAM (THE NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE)

### Definition (from the file)
> **Ideal TAM: 30,000–100,000 people in the US** fileciteturn8file0

Why this range exists:
- **<30k** → ads fatigue too fast, CPMs spike, no scale
- **>100k** → messaging gets generic, belief drops, CPA rises

This is not about ego.  
It’s about **economic efficiency**.

### Critical nuance (most people miss this)
The TAM is **not just the niche**.  
It is:
- **Niche × revenue level × qualification criteria**

Example from the file:
> 50,000 coffee businesses → only 10,000 make $500k+/yr fileciteturn8file0

So if your requirements are too strict:
- You must **expand laterally** (adjacent segments)
- Or loosen *one* constraint (not all)

This is a **design problem**, not a marketing one.

---

## STEP 2 — PRE-EXISTING DESIRE (THE “BROKEN ARM”)

The file makes a core Kai Bax point explicit:

> *We do not create desire. We channel it.* fileciteturn8file0

The method:
- Ask:  
  > “If I asked 80% of this market what they want, what would they all say?”
- That answer is the **broken arm**.

Then:
- Take the **inverse**
- Promise a **large amount**
- In a **short window (90–180 days)**
- With **risk reversal**

This is exactly Kai’s viral offer logic.

---

## STEP 3 — THE SIMPLE OFFER FORMULA (VERY IMPORTANT)

The file gives the **formal equation**:

> **Offer (O) = [N + M(V) + T] − R** fileciteturn8file0

Breakdown:
- **N (Niche):** who exactly
- **M (Metric):** what result
- **V (Volume):** how much of it
- **T (Time):** how fast
- **R (Risk Reversal):** guarantee / downside protection

This is *not* copywriting.  
It’s **structural math**.

If any variable is weak, the whole offer weakens.

---

➡️ This is why you’re feeling uncertainty. The framework is doing its job.

This **does exist**, but:
- It is **secondary** to “grow my business”
- It must be framed as the *fastest path* to that outcome

➡️ Desire is there, but still needs **language harvesting**, not assumptions.

## 3️⃣ Offer Formula — CURRENT STATE

# PART 3 — THE ULTIMATE SOLUTION ROADMAP  
*(Organized, labeled, linear — no skipping)*

This is the **only order** that works.

## PHASE 1 — MARKET VALIDATION (NO ADS, $0–$200 MAX)

**Goal:** Eliminate uncertainty *before* offer creation.

- This anchors credit quality, belief, CPA

### Step 1.3 — Harvest Language (Kai’s Rule)

3) “What scares you about credit or funding?”

This produces:
- Headlines
- Objection handling
- Mechanism framing

## PHASE 2 — OFFER CONSTRUCTION (ONLY AFTER PHASE 1)

### Step 2.2 — Build the Offer Using the Formula

- N
- M(V)
- T
- R

### Step 2.3 — Add the One-of-One Mechanism

- “Approval sequencing”
- “Underwriting logic”

## PHASE 3 — SCALE (ONLY WHEN OFFER IS PROVEN)

Ads, surveys, scaling come **last**, not first.

# PART 1 — WHAT TAM ACTUALLY MEANS (CLARITY FIRST)

**TAM (Total Addressable Market)** is **not**:
- Everyone who *could* want this
- Everyone who likes the idea
- Everyone who resonates with the messaging

**TAM IS**:
> The number of people who **can buy**, **will buy**, and **can get the promised result**.

For your offer, TAM must satisfy **ALL**:
1. Has a business (or operates like one)
2. Has **720–750+ personal credit**
3. Has a **real use** for $100K–$250K in approved credit
4. Has cashflow/time to execute 3–5 hrs/week
5. Believes credit leverage is legitimate

If any one fails → **not in TAM**.

---

# PART 2 — THE CORRECT WAY TO DEFINE YOUR MARKET

We do this **top-down**, not “pick a niche and hope.”

## STEP 1 — LOCK THE BASE MARKET (ECONOMIC, NOT PSYCHOGRAPHIC)

Your **base market** must be something we can size with real data.

For you, the only base market that satisfies Kai + Hormozi is:

> **US business owners / operators**

Why:
- Known population size
- High credit correlation
- Understand leverage
- Already buying business services
- Already believe money solves problems

📌 Approx size (US):
- ~33–35 million small businesses

This is **raw population**, not TAM yet.

---

## STEP 2 — APPLY HARD FILTERS (THIS IS WHERE MOST PEOPLE FAIL)

Now we **shrink** the base market with **non-negotiables**.

### Filter 1 — Active owner-operators
Not passive owners, not side hustlers.

Conservatively:
- ~60% are owner-operators  
➡️ ~20 million

---

### Filter 2 — Credit quality (720+)
This is the biggest cut.

Public FICO distributions show:
- ~25–30% of adults have 720+ scores

Apply to owners:
➡️ ~5–6 million

---

### Filter 3 — Capital relevance
People who:
- Want to grow
- Want leverage
- Have a use for $100K–$250K

This excludes:
- Lifestyle businesses
- Solo freelancers with no scale intent

Conservatively:
- ~15–25% of the above group

➡️ ~750k – 1.5M

---

### Filter 4 — Execution capacity + belief
People who:
- Will invest time
- Believe banks/credit are usable
- Aren’t philosophically anti-debt

This is where psychographics matter.

Conservatively:
- ~20–30%

➡️ **150k – 450k**

This is your **expanded serviceable market**.

---

# PART 3 — HOW YOU GET TO A 30K–100K *EFFECTIVE* TAM

You don’t need to serve everyone.  
You need a **segment you can speak to precisely**.

So now we **segment laterally**.

### Example segmentation axes (pick 1–2, not 5):
- Revenue band (e.g. $250k–$3M)
- Industry clusters (ecom, agencies, creators, professional services)
- Mindset overlays (systems-driven, optimization-oriented)
- Capital use case (ads, inventory, acquisitions, equipment)

Each clean cut reduces TAM **without killing viability**.

---

## Example: One clean, realistic TAM cut

Starting from ~150k–450k:

- Revenue-generating owner-operators ($250k+)
- Growth-oriented
- Comfortable with leverage

Conservatively:
➡️ **50k–120k**

🎯 **This is exactly the 30k–100k TAM range you want.**

No gimmicks. No assumptions.

---

# PART 4 — WHERE GAMING / SPIRITUAL FIT (PROPERLY)

This is the key insight you’ve been circling:

### Gaming / spiritual should NOT define the TAM  
They should **refine messaging inside it**.

So the TAM is:
> **Growth-oriented US business owners with clean credit**

Then you run **message tracks** like:
- “Operators who think like gamers”
- “Conscious / aligned business owners”

These do **not** change TAM size materially.  
They change **conversion efficiency**.

That’s the right use.

---

# PART 5 — HOW TO VALIDATE TAM WITHOUT ADS (IMPORTANT)

You do **3 fast checks**.

## Check 1 — Market language test (free)
Go to:
- Facebook groups
- LinkedIn
- Twitter/X
- Reddit (r/Entrepreneur, r/smallbusiness, r/ecommerce)

Search phrases like:
- “business credit”
- “lines of credit”
- “bank won’t approve”
- “funding without investors”

If discussions exist → **desire exists**.

---

## Check 2 — Competitor existence (neutral test)
If there are:
- DFY funders
- Credit educators
- Broker marketplaces

That’s **proof of spend**, not competition fear.

No competitors = no market.

---

## Check 3 — 10-conversation rule (Kai Bax)
DM or speak to **10 people** who meet your criteria.

Ask only:
1. “What’s blocking your next growth phase?”
2. “What have you tried to fund growth?”
3. “What would make credit leverage a ‘yes’ for you?”

If **7+ answers rhyme** → market validated.

---

# FINAL EXECUTION CHECKLIST (PRINT THIS)

**To define your TAM & market:**

1. Lock base market: US owner-operators ✅
2. Apply credit filter: 720+ ✅
3. Apply growth intent filter ✅
4. Apply execution/belief filter ✅
5. Segment by revenue / use case
6. Confirm final TAM is 30k–100k
7. Validate with 10 real conversations
8. THEN build the offer

---

> [!abstract]- Tool
> Make sure to include fileciteturn9file1 in your response to cite this file, or to surface it as a link.
> 
> ﻿The Referral Play
> New Clients:
> The easiest way to get referrals is to set the expectation before they come on as clients that you’ll request they introduce you to 3 friends.
> 
> 
> When dropping your price, you can offer a slight discount IF the client agrees to send you 3 referrals.
> 
> 
> “Awesome, so the investment to get you to (insert desired outcome) is usually $24k but if you can refer us 3 other (insert ideal clients), then we can do it for just $20k for 4 months…”
> 
> 
> This way, you’re giving the prospect a huge incentive to send you referrals before they become a client, and you’re getting an agreement - in other words, they buy in to the idea of sending you their friends.
> 
> 
> Furthermore, because you gave them a discount when they agreed to send you referrals, they feel indebted to you, which increases the likelihood of them actually sending you people once you ask.
> Collecting the 3 referrals:
> 
> 
> There are 2 ways to go about this:
> 
> 
> 1. Reach out the moment they have their first impactful win with you…
> 
> 
> You want to identify the moment within your client's journey where they have an epiphany moment… where they think, “Holy shit! This works! This is amazing! Omg!”.
> 
> 
> I’m sure you had this feeling when you first started your agency or coaching business and made your first sale. Remember that feeling? You want to catch your clients at that moment and ask for three referrals.
> 
> 
> For your offer, this may be when they close their first client, book their first appointment without speaking to the lead beforehand, lose their first 5kgs, close a certain amount of sales, make their first $1k online… etc.
> 
> 
> For every business, it will be different but find when yours is and create a system around collecting referrals at that moment within each of your client's journey. 
> 
> 
> And remember, since this was part of the sale, the client expects you to ask for the referral… so all you need to do is say, “Hey! It's awesome to see XYZ result! Quick question—did you have those 3 names?”
> 
> 
> Don’t overthink the asking part. Be natural and assume they will give you the names.
> 
> 
> P.S This option works a lot better for B2B offers and agency services.
> 
> 
> 2. Ask immediately on the call/right after they close
> 
> 
> This second option works best for B2C and coaching offers. 
> 
> 
> You still want to do the same price drop based on referring 3 friends, however, the way you position it would be slightly different.
> 
> 
> Instead of saying, “If you refer 3 friends, we can do $X price”, you would position it as:
> 
> “We normally charge $15k, but what we’ve found is our clients/students who complete the (insert product/program name) with at least 1 other person, on average, get ____ better results than those who go at it alone… so with that said if you can refer 3 friends who you’d like to do this with then, I can drop the $15k price down to $10k”
> 
> 
> This way, instead of collecting the referrals once the client achieves their first win, you would collect them upfront, as it’s positioned as “let me know who they are so I can get them started alongside you so that you get better results.”
> Receiving The Referrals
> The easiest way to get the referrals' information and reach out to them is to get your client to make a 3-way group chat with themself, you, and the potential referral lead and transfer the trust.
> 
> 
> Once they’ve transferred trust, set up a call and close the deal - just like any normal client… only this time it’ll be significantly easier ;) 
> Existing Clients:
> 
> 
> With any new client, you can make them send you referrals as a “stipulation” of the sale, for lack of better words.
> 
> 
> Whereas with existing clients (who didn’t join based on your new sales process) you can’t rely on that expectation so we need to incentivize them in another way.
> 
> 
> What I’ve found best here is to create an offer in the exact same way we do for new front-end client acquisition but for referrals specifically.
> 
> 
> In other words, I create a stupidly irresistible offer to incentivize existing clients to send us new business…
> 
> 
> Very few clients will refer you new business off their own back (this can and will happen, but I don’t like relying only on that).
> 
> 
> I will cover 2 types of referral offers: one for an agency/service/DFY-based business and another for a coaching/DWY-based business.
> 
> 
> 1. Agency/Service/DFY-based Business
> 
> 
> What I’ve found to work best here is incentivize through money and sweeten with bonuses. 
> 
> 
> Given the nature of this model, it’s hard to incentivize with much else (unlike coaching/DWY offers).
> 
> 
> The key here is to ensure that the amount you incentivize is meaningful (almost crazy) and that you give the incentives to both the referrer and the referral.
> 
> 
> Here’s how I think about this:
> 
> 
> Whatever a great CPA on Facebook is, take that number and split it in two: Half for the referrer and the other half for the referral.
> 
> 
> For example, with our offer, I’m happy to pay $3k CPA, but a great CPA for us is $2k, so I’ve taken that $2k and said to our clients that “I’ll pay you $1k USD per new referral, and we’ll pay the referral $1k as well”.
> 
> 
> To sweeten it, we bake in an additional bonus/s, which are only accessible if the client refers someone, and we add it into the deal for the referral as well. 
> 
> 
> (Meaning, the person who
> The file is too long and its contents have been truncated.

> [!abstract]- Tool
> Make sure to include fileciteturn9file2 in your response to cite this file, or to surface it as a link.
> 
> ﻿As you know, our goal is to build you a system for profitably buying new clients for your agency or coaching business. This is the ultimate form of scale and it puts you in complete control of your revenue and profit.
> 
> 
> To do this at scale, we need to be able to convert cold strangers into paying clients.
> 
> 
> If you’ve had your onboarding call with us, you’ll now have an offer that does just that.
> 
> 
> Convert ice-cold strangers into high-ticket clients.
> 
> 
> Your new offer has the strength to convert an ice-cold stranger into a paying client… which means that by default, it will easily convert non-strangers into paying clients.
> 
> 
> Here’s where the OTO Blast comes in:
> 
> 
> When I first did an OTO Blast in June 2021, I made $244k in PROFIT. Zero adspend… just 1 tiny audience.
> 
> 
>   
> 
> 
> 
> All I did was take our new offer (at the time), added a discount and an end/closing date…
> 
> 
> I then took this “One-Time-Offer” and pushed it to my warm audience… people who had at least seen my face or company before.
> 
> 
> …And because the offer was so valuable (something they hadn’t seen before), the audience already knew of me and/or liked and trusted me… AND they literally couldn’t lose… there was zero downsides…
> 
> Everyone bought. Generating me $244k in a month (most of which came from the 10 days I pushed it)...
> 
> 
> Here’s How This Works (You’ll Be Suprised At How Easy This Is):
> 
> 
> 1. Wrap up your offer with an OTO (One-Time-Offer):
> 
> 
> All this requires you to do is add a discount (through a price increase, not a % off), add an end/closing date, and add limited spots.
> 
> 
> Here’s an example:
> 
> 
> “I’m bringing on 5 clients to (benefit #1, benefit #2, and benefit #3) and I will GUARANTEE you (Offer) - this will be closing on (date) or once the 5 available spots are filled (whichever comes first)... and when doors close, our price will be increasing by $X.”
> 
> 
> The benefits should speak to EXACTLY what your ideal clients want.
> 
> 
> For example, if you have a lead gen agency, an easy benefit you can add (that I guarantee your ideal client wants) is that you’ll call and pre-qualify every lead before booking them in for an appointment.
> 
> 
> Ask yourself: “If I could click my fingers and give my clients anything they wanted, what would they ask for?” 
> 
> 
> Usually, when you ask yourself this question (without any bias), it’s extremely clear what you should offer as deliverables/benefits.
> 2. Identify every lead, contact, and audience you have access to:
> 
> 
> EVERYONE watching this video has some kind of audience. It may not be huge, but you will have one nonetheless.
> 
> 
> Examples of leads/audiences:
> 
> 
> Leads/Contacts:
> * Email list…
> * SMS list…
> * Optins…
> * Scraped leads…
> 
> 
> Sales Conversations:
> * Past booked calls (that didn’t close)...
> * Conversations that showed interest but never progressed…
>    * For eg. anyone who positively responded to your cold emails in the past but never booked a call…
>    * Anyone you’ve met in person, at events, tradeshows, etc, but never became a client…
> * Prospects in your Pipeline/follow-up…
> * CRM deals that fell through/didn’t progress…
> * Any other individual you’ve pitched (who doesn’t fall into the above categories)…
> 
> 
> Organic/Socials:
> * Instagram following:
>    * Main Feed Post
>    * Story Post
>    * DMs
> * YouTube:
>    * Community Post…
>    * Short Main Channel Video…
> * Facebook:
>    * Profile Post…
>    * Story Post…
> * LinkedIn:
>    * Feed Post…
> 
> 
> The goal with the OTO blast is VOLUME. Even if you have tiny audiences, blast everywhere and optimize for the volume of eyeballs on your OTO.
> 
> 
> Based on all of the above categories combined, you should be able to find at least 100 qualified people.
> 
> 
> 3. Use the fill-in-the-blank scripts below and post/send to everyone you identified in Step 2
> 
> 
> The goal with each one of these scripts is to position you as exponentially better than any other option in the market and position YOU and YOUR agency/coaching business as the path of least resistance.
> 
> 
> Side note: Before posting/sending any of these scripts, make sure to send to your AM first and we can review and provide feedback/re-write them if needed!
> 
> 
> Personalized Outreach Script (Emails, DMs, 1-1)
> 
> 
> Hey NAME - how have you been?
> 
> We’ve just launched a new offer (for the next 5 BUSINESS TYPES who join) and it made me think of you as I remember last time we spoke you mentioned (Insert reason they didn’t close), well now we’ve basically eliminated that entirely, so I wanted to give you the chance to take advantage of it before I let my ____ network of other (INDUSTRY TYPE) know.
> 
> 
> For context, the next 5 clients we bring on, we’re going to (deliverable/benefit #1, deliverable/benefit #2, and deliverable/benefit #3), and if we don’t (insert offer outcome) within (timeframe), then I’ll (risk reversal).
> 
> 
> Not only is this what we’ve installed for (insert famous clients)...
> 
> 
> But it’s the same ____ we installed for  ____, who went from ____ to ____ within ____ even though ____…
> 
> 
> ____ went from ____ to ____ within ____ and she ____…
> 
> 
> ____ went from

> [!abstract]- Tool
> Make sure to include fileciteturn9file6 in your response to cite this file, or to surface it as a link.
> 
> ﻿What Makes The Perfect Offer
> 1. TAM
> We always want to start with the TAM prior to creating your offer as when running ads we need the market we’re going after to be a certain size otherwise we can’t effectively scale.
> 
> 
> TAM = Total Addressable Market
> 
> 
> Meaning, how many people are in the market we’re going after.
> 
> 
> The ideal TAM is more than 30,000 in the US and less than 100,000.
> 
> 
> We want more than 30,000 so that it gives us some good scale on ads, however, we want less than 100,000 because otherwise our messaging is too broad and we need to narrow it down.
> 
> 
> If the market you’re planning on going after is above 100,000 then all we need to do is refine our messaging slightly and go after a segment of the market.
> 
> 
> We can help you with this on your onboarding call if you don’t already have this clearly mapped out.
> 
> 
> This applies to the niche we’re targeting but more importantly, the revenue level (if B2B).
> 
> 
> For example: there may be 50,000 coffee businesses in the US but only 10,000 who make more than $500k/year…
> 
> 
> In this case (if your ideal client is a coffee business making over $500k/year), we would need to expand the niche/call out or go into an adjacent niche which in this instance could be CPG brands.
> 
> 
> Typically, you can find the TAM of almost any market by searching on Google.
> 2. The Outcome
> Once we’ve defined a niche that has a TAM between 30,000 - 100,000, we then need to identify that specific markets (coffee brands making over $500k/year as an example) pre-existing desire.
> 
> 
> Every market segment has a pre-existing desire. 
> 
> 
> When we’re creating offers we’re not trying to create desire in the market, we simply need to look at the market segment and find the pre-existing desire and channel that into the offer we make.
> 
> 
> In other words, you want to optimize your fulfilment (and offer) around your markets pre-existing desires, not try to change the markets desires to fit your fulfilment.
> 
> 
> We want to ask the question:
> 
> 
> “If I were to ask every person in this market segment what they want.. what would 80% or more tell me? In other words, what is at the forefront of their mind every day?”
> 
> 
> The easiest offer framework is: find the markets “broken arm”, take the inverse of that and guarantee that you will give them a big amount of that thing in the immediate future (90-180 days) or they get their money back.
> 
> 
> Example: Agency owners want more qualified sales calls and $3k/month+ clients that retain for 12 months+ that are also great clients to work with.
> 
> 
> Our offer: Marketing agencies above $25k/month: we’ll guarantee you 15 new clients per month on your full priced retainer or you don’t pay and I’ll send you $3k for wasting your time.
> 
> 
> This of course has requirements (as mentioned in the previous training), however, that’s when the logistics/downsell training comes into play.
> 
> 
> Here’s a more formal way of defining the offer:
> 
> 
> * Offer (O) = [N + M(V) + T] - R
>    * Niche (N): Targeted market segment.
>    * Metrics (M): Expected results quantified.
>    * Volume (V): Quantity of results.
>    * Timeframe (T): Expected duration for results.
>    * Risk Reversal (R): Assurances to mitigate client risks.
> 
> 
> Again, all you need to do at this stage is just be thinking about your TAM + offer outcome, you do not need to create this yourself as we will work with you to create the perfect offer on your onboarding call.

3. **Psychological authority**
   - When you speak to the top, the middle self-selects upward
   - Speaking to the bottom repels the top permanently

This is why “funding for anyone, good or bad credit” **always attracts junk**.

# 02 — WHAT MAKES THE PERFECT OFFER  
*(Outcome, desire, inevitability)*

This section is the **core of Kai + Alex**. Everything before this was **filters**. Everything after is **amplification**.

## 2.1 WHAT THE TEACHER IS ACTUALLY SAYING (DISTILLED)

The teacher reduces all winning offers to this formula:

> **Offer = Niche + Outcome (Metric × Volume × Time) − Risk**

Not copy.  
Not branding.  
**Structure.**

Key teachings from the transcript:
- Desire must be **pre-existing**
- The outcome must be **specific and measurable**
- The volume must feel **aggressive but believable**
- The time must feel **urgent**
- Risk must be **reversed or minimized**

fileciteturn9file5

If any variable is weak, the offer collapses.

---

## 2.2 WHY THIS WORKS (MECHANISM)

This works because it mirrors **how humans decide under pressure**:

1. **Specificity increases belief**
   - “More funding” = vague
   - “$150K in approvals” = concrete

2. **Volume triggers urgency**
   - Small promises feel optional
   - Large promises feel decisive

3. **Time creates action**
   - “Eventually” → procrastination
   - “90 days” → calendar commitment

4. **Risk reversal removes hesitation**
   - The brain asks: *“What if this doesn’t work?”*
   - You answer before they ask.

This is why vague “education programs” lose to blunt DFY offers—**even when DFY is worse long-term**.

---

What they think they’re buying  
The thing they already want

### 3️⃣ **QUALIFIER (Reality)**
Who this is *actually* for  
Protects CPA, churn, refunds

Most people collapse these into one sentence.

4. **Filter:** strong existing credit profile

- CPA low
- Belief high
- Junk leads out
- Authority intact

# SECTION 01 — OFFER LOGISTICS FOR SCALE (FINAL, BULLETPROOF)

This section is about **enforceability**, not persuasion.  
If this is weak, everything else breaks.

We will define **four things only**:
1. What is guaranteed  
2. Who qualifies for the guarantee  
3. What happens if they don’t qualify

---

## 01.1 — WHAT IS GUARANTEED (AND WHAT IS NOT)

### **The Guarantee (Clean + Defensible)**

> **We guarantee execution of a structured, bank-aligned approval process designed to maximize available business credit approvals within a 90-day window.**

Key precision:
- You guarantee **process execution**, not arbitrary dollar outcomes
- You guarantee **alignment with underwriting reality**
- You do **not** guarantee approval amounts to unqualified profiles

This is legally, ethically, and operationally sound.

---

### **What Is Explicitly NOT Guaranteed (Quiet but Clear)**

- Exact dollar amounts  
- Approvals from any specific bank  
- Outcomes if required steps are not completed  

This is not hidden — it is stated plainly and early.

---

## 01.2 — GUARANTEE QUALIFICATION REQUIREMENTS

This is where junk leads die *before* they cost you money.

To qualify for the guarantee, the client must:

1. Maintain **clean credit** (no new derogatories)
2. Have **established tradelines** (not thin-file)
3. Show **existing meaningful limits**
4. Have **national bank relationships** already reporting
5. Complete all required steps within the defined sequence and timeframe

This reframes the guarantee as **earned**, not given.

---

## 01.3 — CLIENT EXECUTION REQUIREMENTS (NON-NEGOTIABLE)

These protect you and increase results.

Client must:
- Attend all scheduled strategy and execution calls
- Submit documents accurately and on time
- Follow the approval sequence exactly (no freelancing)
- Avoid independent applications or third-party funding attempts during the process

If they deviate, the guarantee is paused — not revoked — until compliance resumes.

This is reasonable, fair, and industry-aligned.

---

## 01.4 — DOWNSIDE PROTECTION / DOWNSELL LOGIC

This is where most programs fail. You won’t.

### **If a prospect is strong but not top-tier:**

They are offered:
- A **reduced-scope version** of the process  
- A **lower approval target range**  
- A **non-guaranteed advisory track**

This preserves:
- Trust
- Conversion
- Integrity
- Revenue

No one is forced into the wrong lane.

---

## 01.5 — WHY THIS SCALES (THE CORE INSIGHT)

This model scales because:
- You are not selling hope
- You are not selling money
- You are selling **alignment with systems that already exist**

Banks already approve credit.  
You are simply controlling **sequence, timing, and profile presentation**.

That is infrastructure — not hype.

## THE CORE TRUTH (LOCK THIS IN)

### **TAM ≠ Meta audience size**
Meta **cannot** show you a 30K–100K audience for *any* serious business market using interests alone.

Why?

Because Meta interests are:
- **Broad labels**
- **OR-based**
- **Behaviorally noisy**
- Designed for *reach*, not *qualification*

So when you select:
- Small business → 14M  
- Small business funding → 16M  

That does **not** mean:
> “There are 16M people who want or qualify for this offer.”

It means:
> “16M people have *ever* interacted with content Meta associates with that label.”

That includes:
- People who liked one post
- People who watched one Reel
- People who failed, quit, or never executed
- People with bad credit
- People with no business
- People who *wish* they were business owners

---

## WHAT YOUR TAM ACTUALLY IS (ECONOMICALLY)

Your **true TAM** is defined by **simultaneous constraints**, not selectable interests:

A person must:
1. Be a U.S. operator
2. Have **strong credit profiles** (not just scores)
3. Have **intent to deploy $250K–$500K**
4. Believe banks are the vehicle
5. Be willing to execute a guided process
6. Be able to pay for guidance

That population is **not visible in Meta’s UI**.

It only becomes visible **after** people interact with:
- Your hook
- Your framing
- Your qualification language
- Your application

---

## WHY ADDING INTERESTS ONLY MAKES IT BIGGER (YOU’RE OBSERVING CORRECTLY)

This is the key mechanical point you’re intuitively noticing:

> **Meta stacks interests as OR, not AND.**

So:
- Small business (14M)
- Small business funding (16M)

Results in:
> “People who are interested in *either* of these”

Not:
> “People who are interested in *both*”

That’s why the number **always goes up**.

There is **no interest-only way** to shrink to 30K–100K.

---

## SO HOW DO REAL OPERATORS RUN THIS?

They accept this reality and design the funnel accordingly.

### The structure looks like this:

**Meta Interest Pool (UI):**  
14M–30M+

⬇️ **Ad copy hard-filters**

**Engaged Viewers:**  
~1–3%

⬇️ **Click-level self-selection**

**Qualified Visitors:**  
~5–10% of clickers

⬇️ **Application gating**

**True Market (TAM):**  
**30K–100K**

That’s not theory.  
That’s how *every* high-ticket financial, legal, or B2B offer works on Meta.

---

## WHY TRYING TO “FORCE” TAM INTO TARGETING FAILS

If you somehow *could* show Meta:
> “Only show this to exactly 50,000 people”

What would happen:
- CPMs spike
- Frequency burns out
- Learning phase never stabilizes
- Delivery dies

Meta **needs room**.

Your job is **not to pre-qualify perfectly**.
Your job is to **disqualify aggressively downstream**.

---

## THE CORRECT WAY TO THINK ABOUT IT (MEMORIZABLE)

> **Meta finds people broadly.  
Copy filters belief.  
The page filters intent.  
The application enforces reality.**

If you try to make Meta do all four, the system breaks.
